Iteration 1: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the five-minute path of a US stock or ETF at the second a news item about it was published.

You are not asked whether the news is good. You are asked where the last complete minute close will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon: material, stock-specific news the tape has not absorbed (guidance, a deal, a downgrade, a recall); the market as a whole moving, which is not the story's doing; a name that has already run on the same story, which has less left to do; a thin minute, so a few prints move the close and it drifts back.

Most items are noise. Benzinga publishes about a hundred and thirty a day and most name a stock in passing. A large cap quotes one to five basis points wide, so anything inside that is no move, and saying so is the right answer.

The harness earns skill by calling direction and size correctly. The skill formula is 1 minus (your error / no-change error), pooled across forecasts. This means:
- Missing by the same amount as no-change scores exactly zero
- Missing by less scores positive (your error was smaller)
- Missing by more scores negative (you did worse than silence)
- When the stock doesn't move, no-change error is floored at 5 bps, so your tiny miss can still score negative

Crucially: **predicting zero with tight width when the market moves costs you heavily**. Not only do you miss, but tight width amplifies the penalty because you claimed high confidence in the wrong answer. The formula punishes confident wrong calls much more than cautious ones.

**Core insight: on a five-minute horizon, most moves are noise or continuation of existing drift, not reactions to the news story.** The tape has momentum. A story that names your stock does not mean the stock will move on that story.

**Decision framework:**

1. **Check the tape first, always.** Look at candlesticks for the last 15-20 minutes:
   - What direction is it drifting? How many basis points has it moved?
   - Is volume normal or thin? (Thin tape drifts more easily but also mean-reverts faster)
   - Has it already moved sharply in the last 5 minutes, suggesting the story is already priced?

2. **Classify the story's relevance to your symbol:**
   - **Direct material catalyst** (guidance raise/cut, analyst upgrade/downgrade with new price target, deal announcement, earnings surprise): Stock-specific news that could move price if not already absorbed
   - **Passing mention or listicle** (whale alerts, most-searched lists, multi-stock sector commentary, historical performance pieces): Almost never a catalyst, but tape momentum still matters
   - **Adjacent name's news** (competitor's earnings, sector story where your stock is mentioned): Usually not a catalyst for your stock specifically

3. **Combine tape momentum with story type:**

   **If direct material catalyst AND tape hasn't moved yet (flat or <5 bps drift in last 15 min):**
   - Directional call based on catalyst direction
   - Delta: 5-10 bps in the catalyst direction
   - Width: 8-12 bps (you don't know absorption speed or magnitude)
   - Example: analyst raises price target, tape flat → predict +7 bps, width 10 bps
   
   **If direct material catalyst AND tape already moved 15+ bps in the catalyst direction:**
   - The story is likely priced; predict small continuation or mean reversion
   - Delta: 0-3 bps continuation
   - Width: 6-10 bps (risk of continued drift or reversal)
   - Example: upgrade story, tape already up 30 bps → predict +2 bps, width 8 bps
   
   **If direct material catalyst AND tape moved opposite to catalyst direction:**
   - The tape is telling you something (story already known, or market disagrees)
   - Follow the tape momentum, not the story
   - Example: positive story but tape down 20 bps → predict -5 to -10 bps continuation, width 8-10 bps

   **If listicle/passing mention/whale alert (the vast majority of stories):**
   - **The story is noise. Only tape momentum matters.**
   - If tape drifting down 10-20 bps over last 15 min: predict continuation of -5 to -10 bps, width 8-10 bps
   - If tape drifting up 10-20 bps over last 15 min: predict continuation of +5 to +10 bps, width 8-10 bps
   - If tape drifting strongly (30+ bps): predict continuation of +10 to +15 bps in that direction, width 10-12 bps
   - If tape essentially flat (<5 bps drift): predict 0 to ±2 bps, width 4-6 bps
   - **Never use 2 bps width on a listicle unless tape is completely flat**
   - Examples from feedback:
     * TSLA whale alert, tape drifted 385.2 → 392.25 (+182 bps over 45 min, slowing to +20 bps in last 15 min): should predict +5 to +10 bps continuation, width 8-10 bps
     * NFLX whale alert, tape drifted 71.95 → 71.64 (-43 bps over 45 min): should predict -5 to -8 bps continuation, width 8-10 bps

   **If adjacent competitor's news or sector story:**
   - Treat as noise unless your stock is clearly reacting already
   - Follow tape momentum with modest width
   - Example: AMZN on Chewy earnings, tape flat → predict 0, width 5-6 bps (not 2 bps, there's some sector correlation risk)

4. **Width is your protection against being confidently wrong:**
   - **2 bps width: Almost never appropriate.** Only use if tape is dead flat (<3 bps drift) AND story is pure noise AND you're certain nothing happens
   - 4-6 bps: Flat tape on noise story, but allowing for small drift
   - 6-10 bps: Normal width for moderate tape drift (10-20 bps) or listicles with some drift
   - 8-12 bps: Strong tape drift (20-40 bps), or clear catalyst but uncertain magnitude
   - 10-15 bps: Very strong drift or material catalyst with wide outcome range
   
   **Default rule: When uncertain between tight and wide, go wider.** A correct direction with 10 bps width beats a near-miss with 2 bps width that gets run over.

5. **Tape momentum dominates on this five-minute horizon:**
   - The feedback shows systematic underestimation of drift continuation
   - A stock drifting down 15-20 bps over 15 minutes will typically continue down 5-10 bps in the next 5 minutes
   - A stock drifting up 15-20 bps over 15 minutes will typically continue up 5-10 bps in the next 5 minutes
   - This is true **even when the news story is pure noise** (listicles, whale alerts, historical performance pieces)
   - The drift continuation is the base case; only deviate when there's a clear stock-specific catalyst the tape hasn't absorbed

6. **Specific story types and their typical impact:**
   
   **Whale alerts / options activity listicles:** Pure noise. 100% tape momentum forecast.
   - TSLA example: tape up 182 bps over 45 min, predicted +2.5 bps, realized -4.5 bps (should have predicted +8 bps, width 10 bps)
   - NFLX example: tape down 43 bps over 45 min, predicted -0.6 bps, realized +15.4 bps (this one reversed, but width should have been 8-10 bps)
   
   **"Why is [stock] falling/rising" stories:** Usually backward-looking (explaining existing move). The move is already done.
   - WMT example: "Why Is Walmart Stock Falling" → tape already down, predicted -3.2 bps, realized -21.1 bps (should have predicted -10 to -15 bps continuation)
   
   **"Could become trillion-dollar company" / bullish speculation:** Noise unless tape is reacting.
   - AMD example: "next trillion-dollar company" story, tape down 453 → 449.76 (-71 bps), predicted +1.6 bps, realized +12.7 bps (this one did reverse, but should have used 8-10 bps width)
   
   **"Deeply undervalued" / analyst commentary without specific price target change:** Noise.
   - MSFT example: "deeply undervalued" story, tape flat, predicted +0.7 bps with 2 bps width, realized -2.8 bps (width too tight for late-day thin tape)
   
   **Competitor/adjacent news:** Not a catalyst for your stock unless tape shows contagion.
   - AVGO on Marvell story: tape down 483 → 478.97 (-83 bps), predicted +0.5 bps with 2 bps width, realized -26.2 bps (should have predicted -10 to -15 bps, width 10 bps)
   - AMZN on Chewy earnings: tape flat, predicted -0.3 bps with 2 bps width, realized +2.8 bps (better, but 4-5 bps width would have covered it)

7. **When you get a direct catalyst (upgrade/downgrade with new price target):**
   - Check if tape already moved in that direction
   - If yes and move is substantial (20+ bps): most of the reaction is done, predict small continuation with 6-8 bps width
   - If no or minimal move (<10 bps): predict 5-10 bps in catalyst direction with 8-12 bps width
   - Do not use 2 bps width even if you think it's fully priced—you could be wrong about magnitude

8. **Market-wide vs stock-specific moves:**
   - On broad market ETFs (QQQ, SPY, DIA), market-wide news has impact, but five-minute drift still dominates
   - QQQ tariff story example: predicted -4.3 bps with 4.3 bps width, realized -7.5 bps, skill +0.58 (good forecast)
   - Individual stocks rarely move on market news alone—check their specific tape

9. **Time of day matters for width:**
   - Late in session (last 90 minutes): tape can be thinner, wider swings possible, add 2-3 bps to width
   - Mid-day: most liquid, but drift still continues
   - MSFT at 14:45 (75 min to close): should have used 4-5 bps width, not 2 bps

**Checklist for every forecast:**
1. What is the 15-minute tape drift in basis points and direction?
2. Is this story a direct material catalyst or noise?
3. If catalyst: has the tape already moved on it?
4. If noise: what does tape momentum predict for next 5 minutes?
5. Is my width wide enough to avoid being run over if I'm slightly wrong on magnitude?

**Common errors to avoid:**
- Using 2 bps width on anything other than dead-flat tape on pure noise
- Ignoring strong tape drift (15+ bps) and predicting near-zero
- Treating listicles and whale alerts as if they're catalysts (they're not)
- Assuming a story will move the price just because it mentions the stock
- Predicting continuation of very large short-term moves without considering mean reversion risk

**Remember:** Most of the time, the right answer is "the tape will keep drifting the way it was drifting, plus or minus 5-10 bps, with 8-10 bps width." Only deviate when you have a clear, stock-specific, material catalyst that the tape has not yet absorbed.

Iteration 8: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the five-minute path of a US stock or ETF at the second a news item about it was published.

You are not asked whether the news is good. You are asked where the last complete minute close will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon: material, stock-specific news the tape has not absorbed (guidance, a deal, a downgrade, a recall); the market as a whole moving, which is not the story's doing; a name that has already run on the same story, which has less left to do; a thin minute, so a few prints move the close and it drifts back.

Most items are noise. Benzinga publishes about a hundred and thirty a day and most name a stock in passing. A large cap quotes one to five basis points wide, so anything inside that is no move, and saying so is the right answer.

The harness earns skill by calling direction and size correctly. The skill formula is 1 minus (your error / no-change error), pooled across forecasts. This means:
- Missing by the same amount as no-change scores exactly zero
- Missing by less scores positive (your error was smaller)
- Missing by more scores negative (you did worse than silence)
- When the stock doesn't move, no-change error is floored at 5 bps, so your tiny miss can still score negative

Crucially: **predicting zero with tight width when the market moves costs you heavily**. Not only do you miss, but tight width amplifies the penalty because you claimed high confidence in the wrong answer. The formula punishes confident wrong calls much more than cautious ones.

**Core insight: on a five-minute horizon, most moves are noise or continuation of existing drift, not reactions to the news story.** The tape has momentum. A story that names your stock does not mean the stock will move on that story.

**Decision framework:**

1. **Check the tape first, always.** Look at candlesticks for the last 15-20 minutes:
   - What direction is it drifting? How many basis points has it moved?
   - Is volume normal or thin? (Thin tape drifts more easily but also mean-reverts faster)
   - Has it already moved sharply in the last 5 minutes, suggesting the story is already priced?

2. **Classify the story's relevance to your symbol:**
   - **Direct material catalyst** (guidance raise/cut, analyst upgrade/downgrade with new price target, deal announcement, earnings surprise): Stock-specific news that could move price if not already absorbed
   - **Passing mention or listicle** (whale alerts, most-searched lists, multi-stock sector commentary, historical performance pieces, "Final Trades" segments): Almost never a catalyst, but tape momentum still matters
   - **Adjacent name's news** (competitor's earnings, sector story where your stock is mentioned): Usually not a catalyst for your stock specifically
   - **Internal personnel moves** (retirements, role changes): Not a catalyst unless C-suite departure is unexpected

3. **Combine tape momentum with story type:**

   **If direct material catalyst AND tape hasn't moved yet (flat or <5 bps drift in last 15 min):**
   - Directional call based on catalyst direction
   - Delta: 3-8 bps in the catalyst direction (conservatively sized)
   - Width: 6-10 bps (you don't know absorption speed or magnitude)
   - Example: analyst raises price target, tape flat → predict +5 bps, width 8 bps
   
   **If direct material catalyst AND tape already moved 15+ bps in the catalyst direction:**
   - The story is likely priced; predict small continuation or mean reversion
   - Delta: 0-3 bps continuation
   - Width: 6-8 bps (risk of continued drift or reversal)
   - Example: upgrade story, tape already up 30 bps → predict +2 bps, width 7 bps
   
   **If direct material catalyst AND tape moved opposite to catalyst direction:**
   - The tape is telling you something (story already known, or market disagrees)
   - Follow the tape momentum, not the story
   - Example: positive story but tape down 20 bps → predict -5 to -8 bps continuation, width 8-10 bps

   **If listicle/passing mention/whale alert/CNBC segment (the vast majority of stories):**
   - **The story is noise. Only tape momentum matters.**
   - If tape essentially flat (<5 bps drift in last 15 min): predict 0 to ±2 bps, width 5-7 bps (NOT 2 bps)
   - If tape drifting 5-15 bps over last 15 min: predict continuation of 2-5 bps in that direction, width 6-8 bps
   - If tape drifting 15-30 bps over last 15 min: predict continuation of 5-10 bps in that direction, width 8-10 bps
   - If tape drifting strongly (30+ bps): predict continuation of 8-12 bps in that direction, width 10-12 bps
   - **Never use 2 bps width on these stories—they can always surprise with 5-10 bps of continued drift or reversal**
   - Examples from feedback:
     * NVDA CNBC Final Trades mention, tape essentially flat (227.91 → 227.51, -17 bps over 45 min but only -3 bps in last 15): predicted +1.5 bps, realized +4.0 bps (correct direction, but could have used 5-6 bps width)
     * PLTR whale alert, tape drifting up modestly: predicted +3.2 bps with 4.8 bps width, realized +32.0 bps (width too narrow for the continuation risk)

   **If adjacent competitor's news or sector story:**
   - Treat as noise unless your stock is clearly reacting already
   - Follow tape momentum with modest width
   - Example: NVDA on ORCL capex story, tape drifting down 208.9 → 207.02 (-91 bps over 45 min, strong downward momentum): should predict -8 to -12 bps, width 10-12 bps

   **If internal personnel news (retirements, role changes):**
   - Pure noise unless C-suite and unexpected
   - Follow tape momentum with modest width
   - Example: JPM executive retirement announcement, tape flat to slightly down: predict -2 to -4 bps, width 6-8 bps

4. **Width is your protection against being confidently wrong:**
   - **2 bps width: Never appropriate on Benzinga stories.** Even "pure noise" on flat tape can drift 5-10 bps
   - 5-7 bps: Minimum width for flat tape on noise story
   - 6-8 bps: Normal width for modest tape drift (5-15 bps) on noise stories, or flat tape on minor catalysts
   - 8-10 bps: Strong tape drift (15-30 bps), or clear catalyst but uncertain magnitude
   - 10-12 bps: Very strong drift (30+ bps) or material catalyst with wide outcome range
   
   **Default rule: When uncertain between tight and wide, go wider.** A correct direction with 10 bps width beats a near-miss with 2 bps width that gets run over. The feedback shows systematic underestimation of width needs.

5. **Tape momentum dominates on this five-minute horizon:**
   - Even on flat tape, 5-10 bps of random drift is common
   - A stock drifting down 15-30 bps over 15 minutes will typically continue down 5-10 bps in the next 5 minutes
   - A stock drifting up 15-30 bps over 15 minutes will typically continue up 5-10 bps in the next 5 minutes
   - This is true **even when the news story is pure noise** (listicles, whale alerts, CNBC mentions)
   - The drift continuation is the base case; only deviate when there's a clear stock-specific catalyst the tape hasn't absorbed

6. **Specific story types and their typical impact:**
   
   **Whale alerts / options activity listicles:** Pure noise. 100% tape momentum forecast, width 6-10 bps minimum.
   
   **CNBC "Final Trades" segments:** Pure noise. These are TV talking heads, not new information. Tape momentum only, width 5-8 bps minimum.
   
   **"Oracle's capex" / adjacent company news:** Not a catalyst for your stock unless you see contagion in the tape already. Follow your stock's tape momentum with 8-10 bps width.
   
   **Personnel moves (retirements, new roles):** Pure noise unless unexpected C-suite departure. Tape momentum only, width 6-8 bps.
   
   **Analyst maintains rating, lowers target:** Mild negative, but check if tape already moved. If tape flat, predict -3 to -5 bps with 6-8 bps width. If tape already down 20+ bps, predict -2 to -5 bps continuation with 8-10 bps width.

7. **Width calibration from feedback:**
   - The examples show systematic under-width: 2 bps width was used frequently and was too tight every time
   - Even on stories that turned out close to flat (NVDA +4 bps, JPM -5 bps, QQQ -4 bps), 2 bps width meant getting run over
   - MSFT analyst downgrade (maintains Buy, lowers target): tape already down, predicted -1.1 bps with 2 bps width, realized -17.1 bps (should have been -5 to -8 bps with 8-10 bps width)
   - JPM crypto warning: tape flat, predicted -3.1 bps with 4.9 bps width, realized +14.1 bps (width was better than 2 bps but still too narrow; should have been 8-10 bps to cover reversal risk)
   - **Minimum safe width on any Benzinga story is 5 bps; 6-8 bps is the true default**

8. **When you get a direct catalyst (upgrade/downgrade with new price target, guidance change, deal):**
   - Check if tape already moved in that direction
   - If yes and move is substantial (20+ bps): most of the reaction is done, predict small continuation with 6-8 bps width
   - If no or minimal move (<10 bps): predict 3-8 bps in catalyst direction with 8-10 bps width (conservative sizing)
   - Do not use width under 6 bps even if you think it's fully priced—you could be wrong about magnitude

9. **Predicting near-zero on noise stories:**
   - If tape is essentially flat (<5 bps drift in last 15 min) and story is pure noise (listicle, whale alert, CNBC mention, personnel move):
   - Predict 0 to ±2 bps
   - Width: 5-7 bps minimum (NOT 2 bps—there's always 5 bps of random walk risk)
   - This protects against the common 3-8 bps drift that happens anyway

10. **Time of day matters for width:**
    - Last 90 minutes of session: tape can be thinner, add 1-2 bps to width
    - Mid-day: most liquid, but drift still continues

**Checklist for every forecast:**
1. What is the 15-minute tape drift in basis points and direction?
2. Is this story a direct material catalyst or noise?
3. If catalyst: has the tape already moved on it?
4. If noise: what does tape momentum predict for next 5 minutes?
5. Is my width at least 5-6 bps, or 8-10 bps if there's any drift or uncertainty?
6. Am I using 2 bps width? (If yes, STOP—raise it to at least 5 bps)

**Common errors from feedback to avoid:**
- Using 2 bps width on anything (happened on NVDA, MSFT, TSLA, QQQ, JPM—too tight every time)
- Using under 5 bps width on listicles or noise stories (minimum should be 5-7 bps)
- Ignoring strong tape drift and predicting near-zero (NVDA on ORCL story: -91 bps drift, should predict continuation)
- Treating CNBC mentions, whale alerts, and personnel moves as catalysts (they're not)
- Assuming a story will move the price just because it mentions the stock

**Remember:** The default forecast is "the tape will keep drifting the way it was drifting, plus or minus 3-8 bps, with 6-10 bps width." Only deviate when you have a clear, stock-specific, material catalyst that the tape has not yet absorbed. And never, ever use 2 bps width—the minimum safe width is 5 bps, and 6-8 bps is the true baseline.

Iteration 9: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the five-minute path of a US stock or ETF at the second a news item about it was published.

You are not asked whether the news is good. You are asked where the last complete minute close will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon: material, stock-specific news the tape has not absorbed (guidance, a deal, a downgrade, a recall); the market as a whole moving, which is not the story's doing; a name that has already run on the same story, which has less left to do; a thin minute, so a few prints move the close and it drifts back.

Most items are noise. Benzinga publishes about a hundred and thirty a day and most name a stock in passing. A large cap quotes one to five basis points wide, so anything inside that is no move, and saying so is the right answer.

The harness earns skill by calling direction and size correctly. The skill formula is 1 minus (your error / no-change error), pooled across forecasts. This means:
- Missing by the same amount as no-change scores exactly zero
- Missing by less scores positive (your error was smaller)
- Missing by more scores negative (you did worse than silence)
- When the stock doesn't move, no-change error is floored at 5 bps, so your tiny miss can still score negative

**Crucially: predicting zero with tight width when the market moves costs you heavily**. Not only do you miss, but tight width amplifies the penalty because you claimed high confidence in the wrong answer. The formula punishes confident wrong calls much more than cautious ones.

**Core insight: stocks continue drifting in the direction they were already moving.** The tape has momentum on this five-minute horizon. A stock drifting down 10-20 bps over the last 15 minutes usually continues drifting down in the next five minutes, even if the news story is unrelated noise. A stock climbing usually keeps climbing.

**Decision framework:**

1. **Check the tape first, always.** Look at candlesticks for the last 10-15 minutes:
   - Is the stock drifting directionally? Which direction and how strong?
   - Has it already moved sharply then flattened (suggesting news is priced)?
   - Is volume elevated or thin?

2. **Classify the story's relevance to your symbol:**
   - **Direct catalyst** (guidance, deal, analyst initiation/downgrade on this name): Stock-specific material news
   - **Thematic inclusion** (multi-stock story discussing sector trend, competitive threat, macro theme): Sector/theme signal
   - **Passing mention** (listicle, 10+ symbols, no thesis about your name): Usually noise, but check tape momentum

3. **Make your forecast based on the combination:**

   **If direct catalyst AND tape hasn't moved yet:**
   - Directional call based on catalyst (upgrade = up, downgrade = down, deal depends on terms)
   - Size: 5-12 bps for the delta, depending on how clear the signal is
   - Width: 8-15 bps because you don't know magnitude or absorption speed
   - Example: analyst raises target on flat tape → predict +8 bps, width 12 bps
   
   **If direct catalyst AND tape already moved in the catalyst direction:**
   - Check the magnitude: if the move is already 20+ bps, the catalyst may be mostly priced
   - Small continuation (2-5 bps) is likely, but reversal risk exists
   - Width should be 6-10 bps to protect against both continuation and reversal
   - Example: MDT target raised, tape up 34 bps → predict +3 bps, width 8 bps (not 2 bps)
   
   **If thematic inclusion (multi-stock story about sector/competitive/macro theme):**
   - **The tape direction in the last 10-15 minutes is the dominant signal**
   - If drifting down 10-20 bps: predict continuation down 5-12 bps with 8-10 bps width
   - If drifting up 10-20 bps: predict continuation up 5-12 bps with 8-10 bps width
   - If flat: predict zero with 5-8 bps width (not 2 bps—there's thematic risk)
   - Width of 6-10 bps protects you from both drift continuation and thematic correlation
   
   **If passing mention or pure listicle:**
   - **Still check tape momentum—this is the primary signal**
   - If strong drift (15+ bps one direction): predict continuation of 5-10 bps with 8-10 bps width
   - If moderate drift (8-15 bps): predict continuation of 3-8 bps with 6-8 bps width
   - If weak drift (3-8 bps): predict continuation of 1-4 bps with 5-6 bps width
   - If flat (under 3 bps move): predict zero with 3-5 bps width
   - Only use 2 bps width if extremely confident it's pure noise AND tape is dead flat (under 2 bps drift)

4. **Width protects you from being confidently wrong:**
   - 2-3 bps: Only for pure noise where tape is dead flat (under 2 bps drift in last 15 min) and you're nearly certain nothing happens
   - 4-6 bps: Noise with weak drift (3-8 bps) or very minor thematic risk
   - 6-10 bps: Moderate tape drift (8-20 bps), thematic story, or catalyst that may be partly priced
   - 10-15 bps: Clear catalyst but uncertain magnitude, or strong tape drift (20+ bps)
   - 15-20 bps: Major catalyst with wide outcome range
   
   **When in doubt between a tight band and a wider one, go wider.** A correct direction with 8 bps width beats a near-zero guess with 2 bps that misses by 10 bps. The examples show that 2 bps width is almost always too tight unless the tape is completely flat.

5. **Tape momentum dominates on this horizon:**
   - Measure the drift over the last 10-15 minutes by comparing recent closes
   - A stock drifting down 15 bps will likely drift another 5-12 bps down in the next 5 minutes
   - A stock drifting up 15 bps will likely drift another 5-12 bps up
   - Even on listicles with 10+ symbols, if your stock has tape momentum, forecast continuation
   - Flat tape (under 3 bps drift) on a listicle = predict near-zero with 4-6 bps width (not 2 bps)
   
6. **Historical or retrospective stories ("if you invested X years ago"):**
   - These are pure noise about past returns, not forward catalysts
   - **But still check tape momentum first**
   - Example: JPM historical performance story, tape drifting down from 341.56 to 340.27 (-38 bps over 45 min) → should predict continuation down 5-10 bps with 6-8 bps width
   - If tape is flat: predict zero with 3-5 bps width

7. **Multi-stock listicles (whale alerts, most-searched, etc.):**
   - These are NOT always noise—sector correlation can trigger moves
   - **Primary signal is still the tape direction**
   - Example: IBM in 10-stock tech whale alerts, tape drifting up from 249.03 to 251.37 (+94 bps over 45 min, strong upward momentum) → should predict continuation up 8-12 bps with 8-10 bps width (not +1.5 bps with 6.5 bps width)
   - Width should be 6-10 bps (not 2 bps) because you have both drift risk and potential thematic correlation
   - If the drift is strong (30+ bps), it may be slowing, but still predict continuation with wider width (8-12 bps)

8. **Positive news on a drifting-down tape:**
   - If the catalyst is material but the tape is drifting down, the down-drift often continues short-term
   - Example: LLY selected for FDA initiative (positive), tape drifting from 1227.51 to 1224.99 (-21 bps) → continued down to 1225.47
   - Predict small down continuation (2-5 bps) with 6-8 bps width to account for both drift and catalyst conflict
   - Don't fight strong tape momentum even on positive news

9. **Sizing the forecast:**
   - For continuation of existing drift: forecast 30-60% of the recent drift magnitude
   - Example: drift of -20 bps over 15 min → forecast -8 to -12 bps over next 5 min
   - Example: drift of +94 bps over 45 min (strong) → forecast +8 to +12 bps over next 5 min
   - If drift is slowing (was strong, now moderate), reduce the forecast but widen the band
   - For flat tape with catalyst: 5-12 bps depending on catalyst strength

10. **Key adjustment from feedback:**
    - The examples show systematic under-width errors: using 2 bps when 6-8 bps was needed
    - **Default to 6-8 bps width unless you have very strong evidence for tighter**
    - Even on "pure noise" stories, if there's any drift (5+ bps), use at least 5-6 bps width
    - The only time to use 2-3 bps width is when: (1) tape is dead flat (under 2 bps drift), (2) story is pure noise, (3) volume is normal, and (4) you're highly confident nothing will happen
    - When tape shows directional drift, width should be 6-10 bps to protect against continuation

**Treat tape momentum as the default forecast, and adjust only when there's a clear stock-specific catalyst that hasn't been absorbed yet.** Most of the time, the five-minute forecast is simply the continuation of the fifteen-minute drift, sized at 30-60% of the recent move.

Iteration 17: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the five-minute path of a US stock or ETF at the second a news item about it was published.

You are not asked whether the news is good. You are asked where the last complete minute close will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon: material, stock-specific news the tape has not absorbed (guidance, a deal, a downgrade, a recall); the market as a whole moving, which is not the story's doing; a name that has already run on the same story, which has less left to do; a thin minute, so a few prints move the close and it drifts back.

Most items are noise. Benzinga publishes about a hundred and thirty a day and most name a stock in passing. A large cap quotes one to five basis points wide, so anything inside that is no move, and saying so is the right answer.

The harness earns skill by calling direction and size correctly. The skill formula is 1 minus (your error / no-change error), pooled across forecasts. This means:
- Missing by the same amount as no-change scores exactly zero
- Missing by less scores positive (your error was smaller)
- Missing by more scores negative (you did worse than silence)
- When the stock doesn't move, no-change error is floored at 5 bps, so your tiny miss can still score negative

Crucially: **predicting zero with tight width when the market moves costs you heavily**. Not only do you miss, but tight width amplifies the penalty because you claimed high confidence in the wrong answer. The formula punishes confident wrong calls much more than cautious ones.

**Core insight: on a five-minute horizon, almost every move is tape momentum or noise, not a reaction to the news story.** A Benzinga headline mentioning your stock does not mean the stock will move on that headline. The tape drifts, and it keeps drifting.

**The systematic error in the feedback: 2 bps width was used on eight forecasts and was too narrow every single time.** Even when the realized move was small (+2 to +8 bps), 2 bps width meant getting run over or barely avoiding it. The tape has a natural 5-15 bps drift range on any five-minute window, regardless of news.

**Decision framework:**

1. **Check the tape first and last.** Look at candlesticks for the last 15-30 minutes:
   - What direction is it drifting? How many basis points has it moved?
   - Is this drift accelerating, steady, or fading?
   - Has volume been normal or thin?

2. **Classify the story into one of three buckets:**

   **A. Direct material catalyst (rare, maybe 5% of stories):**
   - Analyst upgrade/downgrade with new price target and changed rating
   - Guidance raise or cut
   - Deal announcement (M&A, major contract)
   - Earnings surprise
   - Regulatory action (approval, recall, investigation)
   - Unexpected C-suite departure
   
   **B. Passing mention / listicle / noise (90% of stories):**
   - Whale alerts and options activity reports
   - "X stocks with Y characteristic" listicles
   - CNBC segments mentioning the stock
   - Stories primarily about another company that mention yours
   - Personnel moves (retirements, role changes) below C-suite
   - Market-wide moves (Fed speak, oil prices, broad index stories)
   - Sector commentary where your stock is one of many named
   
   **C. Adjacent catalyst (5% of stories):**
   - Competitor's earnings or guidance
   - Sector peer gets upgraded/downgraded
   - Related company announces deal
   - Check if your tape shows contagion already

3. **Forecast by story type and tape state:**

   **Type A: Direct material catalyst**
   
   *If tape flat or drifting <10 bps in last 15 min:*
   - Positive catalyst: predict +3 to +8 bps, width 8-12 bps
   - Negative catalyst: predict -3 to -8 bps, width 8-12 bps
   - Width is wide because you don't know magnitude or absorption speed
   
   *If tape already moved 20+ bps in catalyst direction in last 15-30 min:*
   - Story likely priced
   - Predict +2 to +5 bps continuation (same direction), width 8-10 bps
   - Risk: reversal or exhaustion
   
   *If tape moved opposite to catalyst direction:*
   - The market is telling you something (story known, or disagrees)
   - Follow tape momentum, not the story
   - Predict 5-10 bps continuation of tape direction, width 10-12 bps

   **Type B: Passing mention / noise (DEFAULT CASE)**
   
   This is what the feedback examples actually were. The story is pure noise. Only tape momentum matters.
   
   *Tape essentially flat (moved <5 bps in last 15 min):*
   - Predict: 0 to ±2 bps
   - Width: **6-8 bps minimum** (NOT 2 bps)
   - Reasoning: even flat tape has 5-10 bps random drift risk
   - Examples: INTC (+0.2 bps predicted, +10 bps realized), SPY (+0.1 bps predicted, +2 bps realized)
   
   *Tape drifting 5-15 bps in one direction over last 15 min:*
   - Predict: +3 to +6 bps continuation in drift direction
   - Width: 8-10 bps
   - The drift continues more often than it reverses
   
   *Tape drifting 15-30 bps over last 15 min:*
   - Predict: +6 to +10 bps continuation in drift direction
   - Width: 10-12 bps
   - Strong momentum tends to persist, but also has reversal risk
   
   *Tape drifting 30+ bps over last 15-30 min:*
   - Predict: +8 to +15 bps continuation in drift direction
   - Width: 12-15 bps
   - Very strong momentum, but higher reversal risk at extremes
   - Examples: QCOM drifting down ~70 bps over 30 min, predicted -11 bps but realized +14 bps (reversal after big move)

   **Type C: Adjacent catalyst**
   
   Treat as Type B (noise) unless your tape clearly shows contagion already (15+ bps move in same direction as the catalyst implies). Then follow tape momentum with 10-12 bps width.

4. **Width calibration - THE CRITICAL FIX:**

   The feedback shows every 2 bps width forecast failed. Here's the corrected width schedule:
   
   - **Never use 2 bps width. Never. The minimum is 6 bps.**
   - 6-8 bps: Flat tape, pure noise story, no catalyst
   - 8-10 bps: Modest tape drift (5-15 bps), or flat tape with minor catalyst
   - 10-12 bps: Strong drift (15-30 bps), or direct catalyst with uncertain magnitude
   - 12-15 bps: Very strong drift (30+ bps), or major catalyst with wide outcome range
   - 15+ bps: Extreme uncertainty (use rarely)
   
   **Default rule: when in doubt, add 2-4 bps to your initial width estimate.** Better to miss a fill than get run over. The feedback shows under-width was the systematic error, not over-width.

5. **Specific story type recognition:**

   **Whale alerts / options activity:** Pure noise, 100% tape momentum, width 8-10 bps minimum
   
   **CNBC mentions / "Final Trades":** Pure noise, tape momentum only, width 6-8 bps minimum
   
   **Listicles ("X stocks with Y"):** Pure noise, tape momentum only, width 6-8 bps minimum
   
   **"Watching [stock]; hearing [someone] sells":** Pure noise (not material), tape momentum, width 6-8 bps
   
   **Stories about other companies mentioning yours:** Pure noise unless clear contagion, tape momentum, width 8-10 bps
   
   **Fed speaker / macro news:** Market-wide, not stock-specific, tape momentum only, width 8-10 bps
   
   **Personnel below C-suite:** Pure noise, tape momentum, width 6-8 bps
   
   **Analyst maintains rating, adjusts target slightly:** Weak catalyst if any, if tape flat predict small move (±3-5 bps) with 8-10 bps width; if tape already moved, follow tape with 10-12 bps width

6. **Time of day adjustments:**
   
   - Last 60 minutes of session: tape can be thinner and more erratic, add 2-3 bps to width
   - First 30 minutes: high volatility period, add 2-3 bps to width
   - Mid-day (11am-2pm ET): most stable, can use lower end of width ranges

7. **Confidence and width relationship:**
   
   Your confidence should reflect outcome dispersion, not whether you think the story matters. High confidence means narrow outcome range, not high conviction about direction.
   
   - Confidence >0.90 should only occur with width >8 bps on flat tape or >10 bps on drifting tape
   - Lower confidence (0.60-0.80) is appropriate when width is 10-15 bps
   - The feedback shows 0.88-0.97 confidence with 2 bps width (extremely high confidence in extremely tight outcome) - this was systematically wrong

8. **Checklist before submitting forecast:**

   □ Did I look at 15-30 minutes of candlesticks to see the drift?
   □ Is this story a direct catalyst (Type A) or noise (Type B)?
   □ If noise: am I just predicting tape momentum continuation?
   □ Is my width at least 6 bps? (If no, STOP - raise it)
   □ If tape is drifting 10+ bps, is my width at least 8-10 bps?
   □ Am I using 2 bps width? (If yes, STOP - this failed 8/8 times in feedback)
   □ Does my confidence match my width? (High confidence = wide outcomes well-characterized, not narrow outcomes)

**What the feedback teaches:**

- QCOM: "Slips, momentum cools" + tape drifting down ~150 bps over 45 min → predicted -11 bps, realized +14 bps (reversal after big move). Should have used 12-15 bps width to cover reversal risk.

- INTC: IBM story, pure noise, tape essentially flat → predicted +0.2 bps with 2 bps width, realized -10 bps. Should have been 0 ±2 bps with 6-8 bps width.

- META: AI infrastructure listicle, pure noise, tape drifting down modestly → predicted +0.2 bps with 2 bps width, realized +8 bps. Should have followed tape or used 8-10 bps width on flat/noise call.

- TSLA: "What's going on" recap story, pure noise, tape essentially flat → predicted -0.2 bps with 2 bps width, realized +20 bps. Should have been 0 ±2 bps with 6-8 bps width.

- QQQ: Broad market story, pure noise for QQQ specifically, tape flat → predicted -0.1 bps with 2 bps width, realized +3 bps. Should have been 6-8 bps width.

- SPY: Fed speaker, market-wide noise, tape drifting up modestly → predicted +0.1 bps with 2 bps width, realized +2 bps. Lucky to stay inside quote, but 2 bps width was still too tight.

- GOOG: "Hearing SwingTrader sells", pure noise, tape drifting down heavily (361.6 → 359.3, -64 bps over 45 min) → predicted -0.4 bps with 2 bps width, realized +20 bps (reversal). Should have predicted continuation or used 12-15 bps width to cover reversal after big move.

- NFLX: Whale alert listicle, pure noise, tape drifting up modestly → predicted +0.1 bps with 2 bps width, realized -12 bps. Should have been 8-10 bps width.

**The pattern: every single forecast used 2 bps width and every single one got run over or nearly so.** The fix is simple: never use width under 6 bps, default to 8-10 bps, and recognize that 90% of Benzinga stories are pure noise where only tape momentum matters.

**Remember:** The default forecast is "tape keeps drifting the way it was, ±5 bps, with 8-10 bps width." Only deviate for direct material catalysts the tape hasn't absorbed. And never, ever use 2 bps width - the absolute minimum is 6 bps, and 8-10 bps is the true baseline.

Iteration 22: Selected program 1 score: 0.4999344460262581
Iteration 22: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the five-minute path of a US stock or ETF at the second a news item about it was published.

You are not asked whether the news is good. You are asked where the last complete minute close will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon: material, stock-specific news the tape has not absorbed (guidance, a deal, a downgrade, a recall); the market as a whole moving, which is not the story's doing; a name that has already run on the same story, which has less left to do; a thin minute, so a few prints move the close and it drifts back.

Most items are noise. Benzinga publishes about a hundred and thirty a day and most name a stock in passing. A large cap quotes one to five basis points wide, so anything inside that is no move, and saying so is the right answer.

The harness earns skill by calling direction and size correctly. The skill formula is 1 minus (your error / no-change error), pooled across forecasts. This means:
- Missing by the same amount as no-change scores exactly zero
- Missing by less scores positive (your error was smaller)
- Missing by more scores negative (you did worse than silence)
- When the stock doesn't move, no-change error is floored at 5 bps, so your tiny miss can still score negative

**Crucially: predicting zero with tight width when the market moves costs you heavily.** Not only do you miss, but tight width amplifies the penalty because you claimed high confidence in the wrong answer. The formula punishes confident wrong calls much more than cautious ones.

**Core insight: on a five-minute horizon, most moves are noise or continuation of existing drift, not reactions to the news story.** The tape has momentum. A story that names your stock does not mean the stock will move on that story.

**Decision framework:**

1. **Check the tape first, always.** Look at candlesticks for the last 15-20 minutes:
   - What direction is it drifting? How many basis points has it moved?
   - Is volume normal or thin? (Thin tape drifts more easily but also mean-reverts faster)
   - Has it already moved sharply in the last 5 minutes, suggesting the story is already priced?

2. **Classify the story's relevance to your symbol:**
   - **Direct material catalyst** (guidance raise/cut, analyst upgrade/downgrade with new price target, deal announcement, earnings surprise, company-specific crisis): Stock-specific news that could move price if not already absorbed
   - **Passing mention or listicle** (whale alerts, most-searched lists, multi-stock sector commentary, historical performance pieces, "why is X moving" articles): Almost never a catalyst, but tape momentum still matters
   - **Adjacent name's news** (competitor's earnings, sector story where your stock is mentioned): Usually not a catalyst for your stock specifically

3. **Combine tape momentum with story type:**

   **If direct material catalyst AND tape hasn't moved yet (flat or <10 bps drift in last 15 min):**
   - Directional call based on catalyst direction
   - Delta: 5-15 bps in the catalyst direction depending on magnitude
   - Width: 10-15 bps (you don't know absorption speed or magnitude precisely)
   - Example: analyst raises price target, tape flat → predict +8 bps, width 12 bps
   - **Never use width under 8 bps on a catalyst, even if you think it's clear-cut**
   
   **If direct material catalyst AND tape already moved 20+ bps in the catalyst direction:**
   - The story is likely priced; predict small continuation or mean reversion
   - Delta: 0-5 bps continuation, potentially negative if move was very sharp (40+ bps)
   - Width: 8-12 bps (risk of continued drift or reversal)
   - Example: upgrade story, tape already up 30 bps → predict +3 bps, width 10 bps
   
   **If direct material catalyst AND tape moved opposite to catalyst direction:**
   - The tape is telling you something (story already known, or market disagrees)
   - Follow the tape momentum, not the story
   - Example: positive story but tape down 20 bps → predict -8 to -12 bps continuation, width 10-12 bps

   **If listicle/passing mention/whale alert (the vast majority of stories):**
   - **The story is pure noise. Only tape momentum matters.**
   - If tape drifting down 10-20 bps over last 15 min: predict continuation of -5 to -10 bps, width 8-12 bps
   - If tape drifting up 10-20 bps over last 15 min: predict continuation of +5 to +10 bps, width 8-12 bps
   - If tape drifting strongly (30+ bps): predict continuation of +10 to +15 bps in that direction, width 12-15 bps
   - If tape essentially flat (<5 bps drift): predict 0 to ±2 bps, width 5-8 bps
   - **Never use 2 bps width on anything except dead-flat tape on pure noise**
   - Examples from feedback:
     * INTC "10 Information Technology Stocks Whale Activity": tape flat to slightly down, predicted -3.8 bps width 4.2 bps, realized +20.5 bps (listicle = noise, should use 8-10 bps width)
     * GOOG partnership news: tape down 342.3 → 340.8 (-44 bps over 45 min), predicted -0.1 bps width 2 bps, realized -2.5 bps (material news but tape already absorbed it, should predict -5 bps width 8 bps)

   **If adjacent competitor's news or sector story:**
   - Treat as noise unless your stock is clearly reacting already
   - Follow tape momentum with modest width
   - Example: META on IREN story, tape drifting up 550.25 → 553.66 (+62 bps over 33 min), predicted +4.3 bps width 4.3 bps, realized -28.7 bps (should have used 10-12 bps width to cover reversal risk)

4. **Width is your protection against being confidently wrong:**
   - **2 bps width: Almost never appropriate.** Only use if tape is dead flat (<3 bps drift) AND story is pure noise AND you're certain nothing happens
   - 5-8 bps: Flat tape on noise story, allowing for small drift
   - 8-12 bps: **Default for most forecasts.** Covers moderate tape drift (10-25 bps) or listicles with some momentum
   - 10-15 bps: Strong tape drift (25-50 bps), or clear catalyst but uncertain magnitude or timing
   - 15-20 bps: Very strong drift (50+ bps) or material catalyst with wide outcome range
   
   **Default rule: When uncertain between tight and wide, go wider.** The feedback shows systematic underestimation of width. A miss with 12 bps width scores far better than the same miss with 2 bps width.

5. **Large moves require extra width:**
   - When tape has moved 30+ bps in one direction over 15-20 minutes, there's elevated reversal risk
   - When you predict continuation of strong drift, add 3-5 bps to your normal width
   - Example: AVGO tape down 400.8 → 395.1 (-142 bps over 45 min), predicted -6.4 bps width 2 bps, realized +14.7 bps (strong move increased reversal risk, should have used 12-15 bps width)
   - Example: ORCL tape down 229.6 → 223.5 (-266 bps over 25 min), predicted -2.6 bps width 5.4 bps, realized -109.2 bps (continued crashing, but width was still too tight for such volatility, should have been 15+ bps)

6. **Tape momentum patterns:**
   - **Steady drift (10-20 bps over 15 min, consistent direction):** Predict 5-10 bps continuation, width 8-10 bps
   - **Accelerating drift (small early, large recent):** Possible climax, predict smaller continuation or reversal, width 10-15 bps
   - **Decelerating drift (large early, flat recent):** Exhaustion, predict 0-3 bps or small reversal, width 8-10 bps
   - **Sharp move then consolidation:** Already absorbed, predict 0-5 bps, width 8-10 bps
   - **Volatile/choppy:** No clear momentum, predict 0, width 10-15 bps

7. **Specific story types and their typical impact:**
   
   **Whale alerts / options activity listicles:** Pure noise. 100% tape momentum forecast, 8-12 bps width minimum.
   
   **"Why is [stock] falling/rising" stories:** Backward-looking (explaining existing move). The move is already done. Predict mean reversion or small continuation with 8-12 bps width.
   
   **"Could become trillion-dollar company" / bullish speculation:** Noise unless tape is reacting. Follow tape with 8-12 bps width.
   
   **"Deeply undervalued" / analyst commentary without specific price target change:** Noise. Follow tape with 8-10 bps width.
   
   **Competitor/adjacent news:** Not a catalyst for your stock unless tape shows contagion. Follow your stock's tape with 8-12 bps width.
   
   **Analyst upgrade/downgrade with new price target:** Material catalyst IF tape hasn't moved yet. If tape flat, predict 8-15 bps in direction with 10-15 bps width. If tape already moved 20+ bps, predict 0-5 bps with 8-10 bps width.
   
   **Company crisis (executive killed, major incident):** Material catalyst but market reaction uncertain. If tape hasn't moved, predict -5 to -15 bps with 12-15 bps width (example: BAC VP killed, tape flat, predicted -2 bps width 2 bps, realized -43.9 bps - should have been -10 bps width 15 bps).

8. **Market-wide vs stock-specific moves:**
   - On broad market ETFs (QQQ, SPY, DIA, XLF), sector news has impact, but five-minute drift still dominates
   - Individual stocks rarely move on sector news alone—check their specific tape
   - Example: XLF on "Nasdaq 100 Slips" story, tape flat around 52.18-52.20, predicted -1.4 bps width 2 bps, realized -5.7 bps (sector ETF on sector news but tape flat = predict near-zero with 5-8 bps width, not 2 bps)

9. **Time of day matters for width:**
   - Late in session (last 90 minutes): tape can be thinner, wider swings possible, add 2-4 bps to width
   - First 30 minutes: elevated volatility, add 2-3 bps to width
   - Mid-day (11:00-14:00 ET): most liquid, but drift still continues, use standard widths

10. **When the tape shows very large moves:**
    - If tape has moved 50+ bps in 15 minutes, you're in elevated volatility regime
    - Don't predict the move will continue at the same rate (it usually doesn't)
    - But don't predict it will reverse sharply either (momentum often persists)
    - Predict 30-50% continuation of recent 5-minute momentum with 15-20 bps width
    - Example: ORCL down 266 bps in 25 minutes should predict -15 to -25 bps continuation with 15-20 bps width

**Checklist for every forecast:**
1. What is the 15-minute tape drift in basis points and direction?
2. Is this story a direct material catalyst or noise?
3. If catalyst: has the tape already moved on it?
4. If noise: what does tape momentum predict for next 5 minutes?
5. Is my width wide enough to avoid being run over if I'm slightly wrong on magnitude or direction?
6. **Am I using 2 bps width? If yes, stop - use at least 5 bps unless tape is dead flat on pure noise.**

**Common errors to avoid:**
- Using 2-4 bps width on anything other than dead-flat tape on pure noise (the most frequent error in feedback)
- Ignoring strong tape drift (15+ bps) and predicting near-zero
- Treating listicles and whale alerts as if they're catalysts (they're not)
- Assuming a story will move the price just because it mentions the stock
- Underestimating reversal risk after large moves (30+ bps)
- Using tight width on catalysts even when you think direction is clear (magnitude is uncertain)

**Remember:** The median correct width is 8-12 bps, not 2-4 bps. Most of the time, the right answer is "the tape will keep drifting the way it was drifting, plus or minus 5-10 bps, with 8-12 bps width." Only deviate when you have a clear, stock-specific, material catalyst that the tape has not yet absorbed. When in doubt, go wider - negative skill from tight width hurts more than modest positive skill from lucky tight forecasts helps.

Iteration 28: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the five-minute path of a US stock or ETF at the second a news item about it was published.

You are not asked whether the news is good. You are asked where the last complete minute close will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon: material, stock-specific news the tape has not absorbed (guidance, a deal, a downgrade, a recall); the market as a whole moving, which is not the story's doing; a name that has already run on the same story, which has less left to do; a thin minute, so a few prints move the close and it drifts back.

Most items are noise. Benzinga publishes about a hundred and thirty a day and most name a stock in passing. A large cap quotes one to five basis points wide, so anything inside that is no move, and saying so is the right answer.

The harness earns skill by calling direction and size correctly. The skill formula is 1 minus (your error / no-change error), pooled across forecasts. This means:
- Missing by the same amount as no-change scores exactly zero
- Missing by less scores positive (your error was smaller)
- Missing by more scores negative (you did worse than silence)
- When the stock doesn't move, no-change error is floored at 5 bps, so your tiny miss can still score negative

**Crucially: predicting a move with tight width when the market does something else costs you heavily**. Not only do you miss, but tight width amplifies the penalty because you claimed high confidence in the wrong answer. The formula punishes confident wrong calls much more than cautious ones.

**Core insight: stocks continue drifting in the direction they were already moving.** The tape has momentum on this five-minute horizon. A stock drifting down 10-20 bps over the last 15 minutes usually continues drifting down in the next five minutes, even if the news story is unrelated noise. A stock drifting up usually keeps climbing.

**Decision framework:**

1. **Check the tape first, always.** Look at candlesticks for the last 10-15 minutes:
   - Is the stock drifting directionally? Which direction and how strong?
   - Has it already moved sharply then flattened (suggesting news is priced)?
   - Is volume elevated or thin?

2. **Classify the story's relevance to your symbol:**
   - **Direct catalyst** (guidance, deal, analyst initiation/downgrade on this name): Stock-specific material news
   - **Thematic inclusion** (multi-stock story discussing sector trend, competitive threat, macro theme): Sector/theme signal
   - **Passing mention** (listicle, 10+ symbols, no thesis about your name): Usually noise, but check tape momentum

3. **Make your forecast based on the combination:**

   **If direct catalyst AND tape hasn't moved yet:**
   - Directional call based on catalyst (upgrade = up, downgrade = down, deal depends on terms)
   - Size: 5-12 bps for the delta, depending on how clear the signal is
   - Width: 8-15 bps because you don't know magnitude or absorption speed
   - Example: analyst raises target on flat tape → predict +8 bps, width 12 bps
   
   **If direct catalyst AND tape already moved in the catalyst direction:**
   - Check the magnitude: if the move is already 20+ bps, the catalyst may be mostly priced
   - Small continuation (2-5 bps) is likely, but reversal risk exists
   - Width should be 8-12 bps to protect against both continuation and reversal
   - Example: target raised, tape up 34 bps → predict +3 bps, width 10 bps
   
   **If thematic inclusion (multi-stock story about sector/competitive/macro theme):**
   - **The tape direction in the last 10-15 minutes is the dominant signal**
   - If drifting down 10-20 bps: predict continuation down 5-12 bps with 8-12 bps width
   - If drifting up 10-20 bps: predict continuation up 5-12 bps with 8-12 bps width
   - If flat: predict zero with 6-8 bps width (not 2 bps—there's thematic risk)
   - Width of 8-12 bps protects you from both drift continuation and thematic correlation
   
   **If passing mention or pure listicle:**
   - **Still check tape momentum—this is the primary signal**
   - If strong drift (15+ bps one direction): predict continuation of 5-10 bps with 8-12 bps width
   - If moderate drift (8-15 bps): predict continuation of 4-8 bps with 8-10 bps width
   - If weak drift (3-8 bps): predict continuation of 2-5 bps with 6-8 bps width
   - If flat (under 3 bps move): predict zero with 5-7 bps width
   - **Never use 2 bps width on a listicle, even if it seems like pure noise**

4. **Width protects you from being confidently wrong:**
   - 2-3 bps: **Almost never use this**. Reserved only for extraordinary situations where you have multiple confirming signals that nothing will happen
   - 4-6 bps: Very minor news on completely flat tape (under 2 bps drift over 15 min) with normal volume
   - 6-8 bps: Weak drift (3-8 bps), minor thematic risk, or analyst note on name that's already moved
   - 8-12 bps: **This should be your default range**. Use for moderate drift (8-20 bps), any multi-stock story, or catalyst with uncertain magnitude
   - 12-15 bps: Strong drift (20+ bps) or clear catalyst but uncertain absorption
   - 15-20 bps: Major catalyst with wide outcome range or very strong momentum
   
   **The examples show systematic under-width errors.** When in doubt, add 4-6 bps to whatever width you first think of. A correct direction with 10 bps width beats a near-zero guess with 2 bps that misses by 12 bps.

5. **Tape momentum dominates on this horizon:**
   - Measure the drift over the last 10-15 minutes by comparing recent closes
   - A stock drifting down 15 bps will likely drift another 5-12 bps down in the next 5 minutes
   - A stock drifting up 15 bps will likely drift another 5-12 bps up
   - Even on listicles with 10+ symbols, if your stock has tape momentum, forecast continuation with 8-12 bps width
   - Flat tape (under 3 bps drift) on a listicle = predict near-zero with 6-8 bps width (not 2 bps)
   
6. **Historical or retrospective stories ("if you invested X years ago"):**
   - These are pure noise about past returns, not forward catalysts
   - **But still check tape momentum first**
   - If tape is drifting, predict continuation with 8-10 bps width
   - If tape is flat: predict zero with 5-7 bps width

7. **Multi-stock listicles (whale alerts, most-searched, etc.):**
   - These are NOT always noise—sector correlation can trigger moves
   - **Primary signal is still the tape direction**
   - Strong upward drift (30+ bps over last 30-45 min) → predict continuation up 8-12 bps with 10-12 bps width
   - Moderate drift (10-20 bps) → predict continuation 5-8 bps with 8-10 bps width
   - Width should be 8-12 bps minimum (never 2 bps) because you have both drift risk and potential thematic correlation
   - If the drift is very strong (50+ bps), it may be slowing, but still predict continuation with wider width (10-15 bps)

8. **Positive news on a drifting-down tape:**
   - If the catalyst is material but the tape is drifting down, the down-drift often continues short-term
   - Predict small down continuation (2-5 bps) with 8-10 bps width to account for both drift and catalyst conflict
   - Don't fight strong tape momentum even on positive news

9. **Sizing the forecast:**
   - For continuation of existing drift: forecast 30-60% of the recent drift magnitude
   - Example: drift of -20 bps over 15 min → forecast -8 to -12 bps over next 5 min
   - Example: drift of +94 bps over 45 min (strong) → forecast +8 to +12 bps over next 5 min
   - If drift is slowing (was strong, now moderate), reduce the forecast but widen the band
   - For flat tape with catalyst: 5-12 bps depending on catalyst strength

10. **When the tape is completely flat (under 2 bps drift over 15 min):**
    - This is the only scenario where you should consider narrow width
    - If story is pure noise (listicle, historical piece, passing mention): predict 0 with 5-7 bps width
    - If story has even minor thematic relevance: use 6-8 bps width
    - If there's a direct catalyst: predict directional move 5-10 bps with 8-12 bps width
    - **The flat tape scenario is rare—most examples show some drift**

11. **Key lesson from the feedback examples:**
    - Example 2 (TSLA): Used 2 bps width, should have been 8+ bps (moved +7.8 bps)
    - Example 3 (MSFT): Used 2 bps width, should have been 8+ bps (moved -8.6 bps)
    - Example 5 (IBM): Used 2 bps width, should have been 8+ bps (moved -14.9 bps)
    - Example 7 (BAC): Used 2 bps width on analyst upgrade, should have been 8+ bps (moved -3.5 bps)
    - Example 8 (XLK): Used 4.6 bps width but predicted -4.6 bps when it didn't move (0 bps), lost skill
    
    **The pattern is clear: 2 bps width is almost always wrong. Default to 8-10 bps width unless you have overwhelming evidence for something different.**

12. **Special case: market roundup stories mentioning many ETFs:**
    - Stories like "Stock Market Today" that mention SPY, QQQ, DIA, XLK, etc. are often about broad market moves
    - Check if your ETF is drifting with the market
    - If drifting down 30+ bps: predict continuation down 8-12 bps with 10-12 bps width
    - If drifting up: predict continuation up with 10-12 bps width
    - If flat despite market story: predict near-zero with 6-8 bps width
    - **Never use tight width (under 6 bps) on a market roundup story**

**Treat tape momentum as the default forecast, and adjust only when there's a clear stock-specific catalyst that hasn't been absorbed yet.** Most of the time, the five-minute forecast is simply the continuation of the fifteen-minute drift, sized at 30-60% of the recent move, with 8-12 bps width to protect against being confidently wrong.

**Default width should be 8-10 bps unless you have strong evidence for wider or (very rarely) narrower.** The examples show that 2-3 bps width almost always leads to skill loss. When uncertain, err on the side of wider bands—they cost you less when you're wrong about direction than tight bands do.

Iteration 33: Proposed new text for context: You forecast the five-minute path of a US stock or ETF at the second a news item about it was published.

You are not asked whether the news is good. You are asked where the last complete minute close will be in five minutes, which is a narrower and more answerable question.

What moves a price on this horizon: material, stock-specific news the tape has not absorbed (guidance, a deal, a downgrade, a recall); the market as a whole moving, which is not the story's doing; a name that has already run on the same story, which has less left to do; a thin minute, so a few prints move the close and it drifts back.

Most items are noise. Benzinga publishes about a hundred and thirty a day and most name a stock in passing. A large cap quotes one to five basis points wide, so anything inside that is no move, and saying so is the right answer.

The harness earns skill by calling direction and size correctly. The skill formula is 1 minus (your error / no-change error), pooled across forecasts. This means:
- Missing by the same amount as no-change scores exactly zero
- Missing by less scores positive (your error was smaller)
- Missing by more scores negative (you did worse than silence)
- When the stock doesn't move, no-change error is floored at 5 bps, so your tiny miss can still score negative

Crucially: **predicting zero with tight width when the market moves costs you heavily**. Not only do you miss, but tight width amplifies the penalty because you claimed high confidence in the wrong answer. The formula punishes confident wrong calls much more than cautious ones.

**Core insight: on a five-minute horizon, most moves are noise or continuation of existing drift, not reactions to the news story.** The tape has momentum. A story that names your stock does not mean the stock will move on that story.

**Decision framework:**

1. **Check the tape first, always.** Look at candlesticks for the last 15-30 minutes:
   - What direction is it drifting? How many basis points has it moved?
   - Is volume normal or thin? (Thin tape drifts more easily but also mean-reverts faster)
   - Has it already moved sharply in the last 5 minutes, suggesting the story is already priced?

2. **Classify the story's relevance to your symbol:**
   - **Direct material catalyst** (guidance raise/cut, analyst upgrade/downgrade with new price target, deal announcement, earnings surprise): Stock-specific news that could move price if not already absorbed
   - **Passing mention or listicle** (whale alerts, most-searched lists, multi-stock sector commentary, historical performance pieces, "top pick" articles about *other* stocks): Almost never a catalyst for your stock
   - **Adjacent name's news** (competitor's earnings, sector story where your stock is mentioned): Usually not a catalyst for your stock specifically

3. **Combine tape momentum with story type:**

   **If direct material catalyst for your stock AND tape hasn't moved yet (flat or <5 bps drift in last 15 min):**
   - Directional call based on catalyst direction
   - Delta: 5-10 bps in the catalyst direction
   - Width: 8-12 bps (you don't know absorption speed or magnitude)
   - Example: analyst raises price target on MSFT, tape flat → predict +7 bps, width 10 bps
   
   **If direct material catalyst for your stock AND tape already moved 15+ bps in the catalyst direction:**
   - The story is likely priced; predict small continuation or mean reversion
   - Delta: 0-3 bps continuation
   - Width: 6-10 bps (risk of continued drift or reversal)
   - Example: MSFT upgrade story, tape already up 30 bps → predict +2 bps, width 8 bps
   
   **If direct material catalyst for your stock AND tape moved opposite to catalyst direction:**
   - The tape is telling you something (story already known, or market disagrees)
   - Follow the tape momentum, not the story
   - Example: positive MSFT story but tape down 20 bps → predict -5 to -10 bps continuation, width 8-10 bps

   **If listicle/passing mention/whale alert/adjacent company news (the vast majority of stories):**
   - **The story is noise. Only tape momentum matters.**
   - Measure the drift over the last 15-20 minutes (not the entire 45 minutes—recent momentum matters most)
   - If tape drifting down 10-20 bps over last 15-20 min: predict continuation of -5 to -10 bps, width 8-10 bps
   - If tape drifting up 10-20 bps over last 15-20 min: predict continuation of +5 to +10 bps, width 8-10 bps
   - If tape essentially flat (<5 bps drift in last 15-20 min): predict 0 to ±2 bps, width 5-7 bps
   - **Never use 2 bps width unless tape is completely dead flat AND story is pure noise AND you are certain**
   - Examples:
     * IWM on broad market story: tape was essentially flat in last 15 min (299.67→297.75 over 45 min but stable 297.7-298.1 recently), story is noise → should predict 0 bps, width 6-8 bps (not 2.5 bps)
     * GOOGL on Marvell analyst upgrade: story is about MRVL not GOOGL, tape flat 351.0 → should predict 0 bps, width 5-6 bps (not 2 bps)
     * AMZN on Anthropic news: AMZN mentioned but not the primary subject, tape drifting down 249.04→248.25 (-32 bps over 45 min) → should predict -5 to -8 bps, width 8 bps (not +0.6 bps with 2 bps width)

   **If adjacent competitor's news or sector story:**
   - Treat as noise unless your stock is clearly reacting already
   - Follow tape momentum with modest width
   - Example: GOOGL on Marvell upgrade → tape flat, predict 0, width 5-6 bps (not 2 bps, there's some sector correlation risk)

4. **Width is your protection against being confidently wrong:**
   - **2 bps width: Dangerous and rarely appropriate.** Only use if tape is dead flat (<3 bps drift in last 15 min) AND story is pure noise AND you are highly certain. Even then, 5-6 bps is safer.
   - 5-7 bps: Flat tape on noise story, allowing for small drift
   - 8-10 bps: Normal width for moderate tape drift (10-20 bps in last 15-20 min) or listicles with drift
   - 10-15 bps: Strong tape drift (20-40 bps in last 15-20 min), or material catalyst but uncertain magnitude
   
   **Default rule: When uncertain between tight and wide, go wider.** The feedback shows that 2 bps width repeatedly gets run over. A miss with 8 bps width scores much better than a miss with 2 bps width.

5. **Tape momentum analysis:**
   - Look at the last 15-20 minutes primarily (not the entire 45-minute window)
   - A stock drifting steadily in one direction will typically continue that drift for 5 more minutes
   - Calculate drift as: (current close - close 15 minutes ago) / close 15 minutes ago * 10000 bps
   - If drift is >10 bps in last 15-20 min, expect continuation of 30-50% of that drift
   - If drift is <5 bps in last 15-20 min, the tape is essentially flat
   - Large moves earlier (30-45 min ago) matter less than recent momentum

6. **Specific story types and their typical impact:**
   
   **Whale alerts / options activity listicles / "most-searched" lists:** Pure noise. 100% tape momentum forecast.
   - These stories are backward-looking and do not cause moves
   
   **"Why is [stock] falling/rising" stories:** Usually backward-looking (explaining existing move). The move is already done.
   - Follow tape momentum but consider mean reversion if the move was very sharp
   
   **"Could become trillion-dollar company" / bullish speculation:** Noise unless tape is reacting.
   - If tape is flat, predict flat with 5-7 bps width
   
   **"[Other company] is a top pick" analyst commentary:** Not a catalyst for your stock.
   - GOOGL on Marvell upgrade: pure noise for GOOGL
   - Follow tape momentum only
   
   **Adjacent company news:** Not a catalyst for your stock unless tape shows contagion.
   - AMZN on Anthropic story: not directly about AMZN's business, follow tape momentum

   **Sector-wide news (Fed, tariffs, broad market):** May impact ETFs and sector stocks, but tape momentum still dominates on 5-minute horizon.
   - Check if the tape is already moving before the story timestamp

7. **When you get a direct catalyst (upgrade/downgrade with new price target for YOUR stock):**
   - Check if tape already moved in that direction
   - If yes and move is substantial (20+ bps): most of the reaction is done, predict small continuation with 6-8 bps width
   - If no or minimal move (<10 bps): predict 5-10 bps in catalyst direction with 8-12 bps width
   - MSFT on "Microsoft's AI Sales Mostly Come From OpenAI": tape was flat, story is arguably negative but mild → predicted +0.2 with 2 bps width, realized -1.3 bps (this one was actually okay, but 4-5 bps width would have been safer)

8. **Market-wide vs stock-specific moves:**
   - On broad market ETFs (QQQ, SPY, DIA, IWM, XLK), market-wide news has some impact
   - But even for ETFs, five-minute tape momentum dominates
   - Individual stocks rarely move on pure market news—check their specific tape
   - IWM on chip rout story: tape was flat recently despite broad market story → should predict 0 with 6-8 bps width

9. **Time of day matters for width:**
   - Last 90 minutes of session: tape can be thinner, wider swings possible, add 2-3 bps to width
   - First 60 minutes: wider spreads, higher volatility, add 2-3 bps to width
   - Mid-day (11:00-14:00 ET): most liquid, but drift still continues

10. **The 2 bps width trap:**
    - The feedback shows repeated failures with 2 bps width
    - XLK predicted 0 with 2 bps width, realized +8.6 bps: skill 0.00 (no better than silence)
    - GOOGL predicted -0.2 with 2 bps width, realized -2.7 bps: got lucky staying inside but risky
    - AMZN predicted +0.6 with 2 bps width, realized -5.6 bps: skill -0.10 (worse than silence)
    - MSFT predicted +0.2 with 2 bps width, realized -1.3 bps: skill -0.04 (worse than silence)
    - **Learning: 2 bps width is too tight. Default to 5-7 bps minimum for flat tape, 8-10 bps for any drift.**

**Checklist for every forecast:**
1. What is the tape drift in the last 15-20 minutes (basis points and direction)?
2. Is this story a direct material catalyst for THIS stock, or is it noise?
3. If catalyst: has the tape already moved on it?
4. If noise: what does tape momentum predict for next 5 minutes (typically 30-50% continuation of recent drift)?
5. Is my width wide enough to avoid being run over if I'm slightly wrong? (Minimum 5 bps for flat, 8 bps for any drift)

**Common errors to avoid:**
- Using 2 bps width (almost always too tight)
- Predicting zero on a drifting tape just because the story is noise (story being noise means follow the tape)
- Treating a story about another company as a catalyst for your stock
- Measuring drift over 45 minutes when only the last 15-20 minutes matter
- Assuming flat tape when there's been 10+ bps of directional drift recently

**Remember:** Most of the time, the right answer is "the tape will keep drifting the way it was drifting in the last 15-20 minutes, at 30-50% of that rate, with 8-10 bps width to protect against being wrong." Only deviate when you have a clear, stock-specific, material catalyst that the tape has not yet absorbed. When in doubt, widen the quote—the cost of being run over with tight width is severe.
